You know, give it two weeks to grow out just a tiny bit so I have something to work with to fix it. Especially bangs. People get a little sad or bored at 2 AM and suddenly decide they need micro-bangs, and it's always a disaster. Everyone thinks they want bangs right now, like those really cute curtain bangs, but they don't realize how much styling it takes. You have to use a round brush and a blow dryer every single morning, or they just lay flat against your forehead and look super greasy. I always try to warn them, but they never listen. They just bring in a picture from Pinterest and say, 'I want this,' and I have to explain that the girl in the picture has a professional stylist and perfect lighting. Lighting changes everything. We have this huge ring light at the salon for taking Instagram photos of our work, and if you stand in front of it without makeup, you look completely washed out. I actually had to buy this special color-correcting primer just so I wouldn't look dead in the background of my coworkers' photos. It's green, which is so weird to put on your face, but it cancels out the redness. Color theory is actually super interesting, it's basically the whole foundation of doing hair color. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, I haven't had a two-week stretch of feeling like this before.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, have you ever had another episode like this that lasted at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I... I think you just asked me that, didn't you? Or wait, did you? My brain is just so foggy right now. But no, I haven't. Not before this time. It's just been this one really long stretch. Kind of like when a client wants to go from box-dye black to platinum blonde in one session. It's just this long, agonizing process, and you're just sitting there waiting for the bleach to lift, hoping their hair doesn't melt off in the foils. Because box dye is the absolute worst. It has all these metallic salts in it that react with professional lightener, and it literally smokes. I saw a girl's hair start smoking once in beauty school. The instructor had to rush over and rinse it out in the bowl, and the smell was horrendous. It smelled like burning tires and sulfur. The bowl area in beauty school was always a mess anyway. The drains were constantly clogged with hair because nobody ever cleaned the traps. It's so gross pulling out those wet clumps of hair. But yeah, no, I haven't had another time like this before. Just this one.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times during this past year and two months when you felt depressed for at least two months at a stretch?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I'd say so. Looking back on it, I've spent most of this whole time... like this whole decline over the last year or whatever... feeling really depressed and just weighed down. There were only short stretches where the sadness wasn't as prominent. It's just so heavy, you know? Like when you try to lift a gallon of developer. We buy developer in these massive jugs at the salon to save money, mostly 20 volume because that's what we use the most of for gray coverage. Gray hair is so stubborn. It lacks pigment, obviously, but the cuticle is also super tight, so you really have to pack the color on and sometimes use heat to open it up. A lot of women come in every four weeks on the dot to get their roots touched up. They hate seeing that skunk stripe down their part. I had this one client, Mrs. Higgins, she would come in every three weeks. She was terrified of anyone knowing she wasn't a natural redhead. Red fades the fastest, too. The color molecules are larger than other colors, so they literally slip right out of the hair shaft every time you wash it. That's why I always tell my redheads to wash in cold water. But who wants to take a freezing cold shower? Especially in the winter. Our water heater at my apartment is terrible, so I end up taking cold showers half the time anyway. It takes like twenty minutes for the water to even get lukewarm. But um... yeah, mostly I've just been really depressed this whole time, with only small breaks.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like a failure or feel bad about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. I just... I keep thinking I'm a burden on everyone. Like I completely deserve all of this happening to me because I couldn't even keep my money straight or hold onto my job. I couldn't even manage basic salon responsibilities, you know? Like sweeping up hair or keeping my station sanitized. State board is so strict about sanitation. If an inspector walks in and sees a single hair on your clippers or a comb sitting on the counter instead of in the Barbicide, they can fine you on the spot. The Barbicide jar has to be changed every single day, and the liquid is this bright, electric blue. It stains everything if you spill it. I spilled it once on my favorite white smock, and it looked like a Smurf bled on me. I tried soaking it in bleach, but then the fabric got all weak and tore right at the pocket. Pockets are so important, too, for holding your clips and shears while you work. My shears cost like four hundred dollars. They're Japanese steel, super sharp. You can slice your finger open so easily if you're not paying attention when you're point-cutting. I did that once and had to superglue the cut shut so I could finish my client's blowout. Superglue actually works really well for paper cuts and stuff, too. But um... what was the question? Oh, yeah, feeling bad about myself. Yeah, I just feel like a total failure because I couldn't keep anything together.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really have. I used to love chatting with my clients, you know? Like, really getting into their lives while I was foiling their hair. And I'd always help out the newer stylists if they were struggling with a formulation. Plus, on my days off, I'd go hiking with my friends all the time. There's this one trail up by the old reservoir that has the most amazing views in the fall. The leaves turn these incredible shades of copper and gold. It's actually a lot like this one custom color I mixed for a client once—half 8G, half 7C, with just a ribbon of red kicker. It was stunning. Color theory is honestly my favorite part of cosmetology. You have to know the color wheel inside and out to neutralize unwanted tones. Like, if someone pulls too warm and their blonde looks brassy, you have to use a violet-based toner to counteract the yellow. Violet cancels yellow, blue cancels orange, green cancels red. It's just basic science, but it feels like magic when you tone a raw, yellow bleach job into this beautiful, icy platinum. We use this one liquid demi-permanent gloss that processes in like, five minutes at the bowl. It's so fast, you literally can't take your eyes off it or it'll grab too dark and look muddy. Muddy blonde is the worst. It just looks dull and lifeless. Which is... I guess kind of how I feel now. Dull. I don't do any of that stuff anymore. I don't hike, I don't help anyone at the salon. I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, yeah, I guess so. I've been skipping meals lately without really meaning to, and then I'll realize at night that all I've had the entire day is coffee and maybe a few bites of something. We drink so much coffee at the salon. The owner actually bought this really fancy espresso machine for the break room because we wanted to offer clients like, cappuccinos and lattes while their color processes. It makes such a huge difference in your tips when you hand a client a nice little latte in a real ceramic mug. I tried learning how to do the latte art, you know, like the little leaves and hearts, but it's actually super hard. You have to steam the milk to the exact right microfoam texture, or it just blobs into the cup. And if the milk gets too hot, it scorches and tastes terrible. Burnt milk is the worst smell. It honestly reminds me of when someone's hair gets fried off by a flat iron that's turned up too high. You always have to use a thermal protectant spray before hot tools. Always. Heat damage is completely irreversible, once the protein bonds are shattered, you literally just have to chop it off. It breaks my heart when a girl comes in with her ends just snapping off in the comb like dry spaghetti. But um... yeah, my appetite. I just forget to eat. I've noticed my clothes have gotten a lot looser, too. Like my favorite jeans just sag on me now, and I have to wrap my work apron around my waist twice just to keep it from slipping down.

Strawberries are actually my favorite fruit, especially in the summer when you can get them fresh from the farmer's market. Like rich chocolate browns, copper, auburn... it's so gorgeous. The formulas are so fun to mix, it's like painting. They're made of this Japanese steel, usually, and they cost like, hundreds of dollars. My first pair was $300, and that was with my student discount. You have to get them sharpened by this special guy who comes to the salon once a month with a little mobile workshop in his van. He's this older guy named Gary, and he always brings donuts. I read somewhere that chin breakouts are hormonal, but I don't know. Skin care is so confusing. There are a million different serums and acids now. Hyaluronic acid, salicylic acid, glycolic... it's overwhelming. Sometimes I just want to wash my face with regular soap, but my esthetician friend would actually murder me if I did that. She says bar soap destroys your skin barrier. But yeah, no, I've never had a time where I didn't need sleep or felt super energized like that. Just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any medical illness or injury involving your brain, like a head injury, stroke, seizure disorder, or thyroid problem?\n\nPatient: Um, no, nothing like that. I've never had a stroke or a seizure or anything wrong with my brain like that. I did hit my head on a cabinet door at the salon once, though. Someone left it open—probably Sarah, she's always leaving the color bar cabinets wide open—and I just stood up right into it. It hurt so bad, I actually saw stars for a second. But I didn't go to the doctor or anything, I just put some ice on it. The ice machine at work is always broken, by the way. It makes this horrible grinding noise, like it's chewing on rocks, and then it just spits out water instead of cubes. We've told the owner, Mr. Davis, like five times, but he's so cheap. He hates spending money on maintenance. He wouldn't even fix the AC last summer until three clients complained that they were sweating through their capes. Those capes get so hot anyway, they don't breathe at all. It's like wearing a trash bag. We try to use the lightweight ones, but they still trap all your body heat. Anyway, no, I haven't had any major head injuries or thyroid things. My thyroid is fine, as far as I know.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of depression, have you ever had another episode of feeling this depressed for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. Not before this current time. I mean, I've had bad days here and there, but never two whole weeks in a row of feeling this down. Two weeks is actually exactly how long I tell my clients to wait if they try to cut their own hair at home and mess it up. You know, give it two weeks to grow out just a tiny bit so I have something to work with to fix it. Especially bangs. People get a little sad or bored at 2 AM and suddenly decide they need micro-bangs, and it's always a disaster. Everyone thinks they want bangs right now, like those really cute curtain bangs, but they don't realize how much styling it takes. You have to use a round brush and a blow dryer every single morning, or they just lay flat against your forehead and look super greasy. I always try to warn them, but they never listen. They just bring in a picture from Pinterest and say, 'I want this,' and I have to explain that the girl in the picture has a professional stylist and perfect lighting. Lighting changes everything. We have this huge ring light at the salon for taking Instagram photos of our work, and if you stand in front of it without makeup, you look completely washed out. I actually had to buy this special color-correcting primer just so I wouldn't look dead in the background of my coworkers' photos. It's green, which is so weird to put on your face, but it cancels out the redness. Color theory is actually super interesting, it's basically the whole foundation of doing hair color. But um... what was I saying? Oh, right. No, I haven't had a two-week stretch of feeling like this before.\n\nDoctor: Before this current depression, have you ever had another episode like this that lasted at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, I... I think you just asked me that, didn't you? Or wait, did you? My brain is just so foggy right now. But no, I haven't. Not before this time. It's just been this one really long stretch. Kind of like when a client wants to go from box-dye black to platinum blonde in one session. It's just this long, agonizing process, and you're just sitting there waiting for the bleach to lift, hoping their hair doesn't melt off in the foils. Because box dye is the absolute worst. It has all these metallic salts in it that react with professional lightener, and it literally smokes. I saw a girl's hair start smoking once in beauty school. The instructor had to rush over and rinse it out in the bowl, and the smell was horrendous. It smelled like burning tires and sulfur. The bowl area in beauty school was always a mess anyway. The drains were constantly clogged with hair because nobody ever cleaned the traps. It's so gross pulling out those wet clumps of hair. But yeah, no, I haven't had another time like this before. Just this one.\n\nDoctor: Have there ever been times during this past year and two months when you felt depressed for at least two months at a stretch?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I'd say so. Looking back on it, I've spent most of this whole time... like this whole decline over the last year or whatever... feeling really depressed and just weighed down. There were only short stretches where the sadness wasn't as prominent. It's just so heavy, you know? Like when you try to lift a gallon of developer. We buy developer in these massive jugs at the salon to save money, mostly 20 volume because that's what we use the most of for gray coverage. Gray hair is so stubborn. It lacks pigment, obviously, but the cuticle is also super tight, so you really have to pack the color on and sometimes use heat to open it up. A lot of women come in every four weeks on the dot to get their roots touched up. They hate seeing that skunk stripe down their part. I had this one client, Mrs. Higgins, she would come in every three weeks. She was terrified of anyone knowing she wasn't a natural redhead. Red fades the fastest, too. The color molecules are larger than other colors, so they literally slip right out of the hair shaft every time you wash it. That's why I always tell my redheads to wash in cold water. But who wants to take a freezing cold shower? Especially in the winter. Our water heater at my apartment is terrible, so I end up taking cold showers half the time anyway. It takes like twenty minutes for the water to even get lukewarm. But um... yeah, mostly I've just been really depressed this whole time, with only small breaks.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like a failure or feel bad about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, all the time. I just... I keep thinking I'm a burden on everyone. Like I completely deserve all of this happening to me because I couldn't even keep my money straight or hold onto my job. I couldn't even manage basic salon responsibilities, you know? Like sweeping up hair or keeping my station sanitized. State board is so strict about sanitation. If an inspector walks in and sees a single hair on your clippers or a comb sitting on the counter instead of in the Barbicide, they can fine you on the spot. The Barbicide jar has to be changed every single day, and the liquid is this bright, electric blue. It stains everything if you spill it. I spilled it once on my favorite white smock, and it looked like a Smurf bled on me. I tried soaking it in bleach, but then the fabric got all weak and tore right at the pocket. Pockets are so important, too, for holding your clips and shears while you work. My shears cost like four hundred dollars. They're Japanese steel, super sharp. You can slice your finger open so easily if you're not paying attention when you're point-cutting. I did that once and had to superglue the cut shut so I could finish my client's blowout. Superglue actually works really well for paper cuts and stuff, too. But um... what was the question? Oh, yeah, feeling bad about myself. Yeah, I just feel like a total failure because I couldn't keep anything together.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really have. I used to love chatting with my clients, you know? Like, really getting into their lives while I was foiling their hair. And I'd always help out the newer stylists if they were struggling with a formulation. Plus, on my days off, I'd go hiking with my friends all the time. There's this one trail up by the old reservoir that has the most amazing views in the fall. The leaves turn these incredible shades of copper and gold. It's actually a lot like this one custom color I mixed for a client once—half 8G, half 7C, with just a ribbon of red kicker. It was stunning. Color theory is honestly my favorite part of cosmetology. You have to know the color wheel inside and out to neutralize unwanted tones. Like, if someone pulls too warm and their blonde looks brassy, you have to use a violet-based toner to counteract the yellow. Violet cancels yellow, blue cancels orange, green cancels red. It's just basic science, but it feels like magic when you tone a raw, yellow bleach job into this beautiful, icy platinum. We use this one liquid demi-permanent gloss that processes in like, five minutes at the bowl. It's so fast, you literally can't take your eyes off it or it'll grab too dark and look muddy. Muddy blonde is the worst. It just looks dull and lifeless. Which is... I guess kind of how I feel now. Dull. I don't do any of that stuff anymore. I don't hike, I don't help anyone at the salon. I just keep my head down and avoid people. I just don't have that spark in me anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. I've been skipping meals lately without really meaning to, and then I'll realize at night that all I've had the entire day is coffee and maybe a few bites of something. We drink so much coffee at the salon. The owner actually bought this really fancy espresso machine for the break room because we wanted to offer clients like, cappuccinos and lattes while their color processes. It makes such a huge difference in your tips when you hand a client a nice little latte in a real ceramic mug. I tried learning how to do the latte art, you know, like the little leaves and hearts, but it's actually super hard. You have to steam the milk to the exact right microfoam texture, or it just blobs into the cup. And if the milk gets too hot, it scorches and tastes terrible. Burnt milk is the worst smell. It honestly reminds me of when someone's hair gets fried off by a flat iron that's turned up too high. You always have to use a thermal protectant spray before hot tools. Always. Heat damage is completely irreversible, once the protein bonds are shattered, you literally just have to chop it off. It breaks my heart when a girl comes in with her ends just snapping off in the comb like dry spaghetti. But um... yeah, my appetite. I just forget to eat. I've noticed my clothes have gotten a lot looser, too. Like my favorite jeans just sag on me now, and I have to wrap my work apron around my waist twice just to keep it from slipping down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It makes such a huge difference in your tips when you hand a client a nice little latte in a real ceramic mug. I tried learning how to do the latte art, you know, like the little leaves and hearts, but it's actually super hard. You have to steam the milk to the exact right microfoam texture, or it just blobs into the cup. And if the milk gets too hot, it scorches and tastes terrible. Burnt milk is the worst smell. It honestly reminds me of when someone's hair gets fried off by a flat iron that's turned up too high. You always have to use a thermal protectant spray before hot tools. Always. Heat damage is completely irreversible, once the protein bonds are shattered, you literally just have to chop it off. It breaks my heart when a girl comes in with her ends just snapping off in the comb like dry spaghetti. But um... yeah, my appetite. I just forget to eat. I've noticed my clothes have gotten a lot looser, too. Like my favorite jeans just sag on me now, and I have to wrap my work apron around my waist twice just to keep it from slipping down."
